### Q1. JEE Main 2026 (08 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Consider the reaction
$2\text{H}_2\text{S}(g) + 3\text{O}_2(g) \rightarrow 2\text{H}_2\text{O}(l) + 2\text{SO}_2(g)$
The magnitude of enthalpy change for the reaction in kJ mol$^{-1}$ is ________. (Nearest integer)
Given: $\Delta_fH^{\ominus}(\text{H}_2\text{S}) = -20.1$ kJ mol$^{-1}$
$\Delta_fH^{\ominus}(\text{H}_2\text{O}) = -286.0$ kJ mol$^{-1}$
$\Delta_fH^{\ominus}(\text{SO}_2) = -297.0$ kJ mol$^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1126**

**Detailed Solution:**

The standard enthalpy of reaction is given by:

$\Delta_r H^{\ominus} = \sum \Delta_f H^{\ominus}(\text{products}) - \sum \Delta_f H^{\ominus}(\text{reactants})$

For the given reaction:
$2\text{H}_2\text{S}(g) + 3\text{O}_2(g) \rightarrow 2\text{H}_2\text{O}(l) + 2\text{SO}_2(g)$

$\Delta_r H^{\ominus} = [2 \Delta_f H^{\ominus}(\text{H}_2\text{O}, l) + 2 \Delta_f H^{\ominus}(\text{SO}_2, g)] - [2 \Delta_f H^{\ominus}(\text{H}_2\text{S}, g) + 3 \Delta_f H^{\ominus}(\text{O}_2, g)]$

Substituting the given values:
$\Delta_r H^{\ominus} = [2(-286.0) + 2(-297.0)] - [2(-20.1) + 3(0)]$

$\Delta_r H^{\ominus} = [-572.0 - 594.0] - [-40.2]$

$\Delta_r H^{\ominus} = -1166.0 + 40.2 = -1125.8 \text{ kJ mol}^{-1}$

The magnitude of the enthalpy change is $1125.8 \text{ kJ mol}^{-1}$.

Rounding to the nearest integer, we get $1126$.

Answer: $1126$

</details>

### Q2. JEE Main 2026 (05 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Consider the following data for the reaction
$X_2(g) + Y_2(g) \rightleftharpoons 2XY(g)$
at $600\text{ K}$. The $\Delta_r G^\circ$ (in kJ mol$^{-1}$) for the reaction is :
<table style="border: 1px solid black; border-collapse: collapse; width: auto;"><tr><th style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">Compound</th><th style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">$\Delta_f H^\circ_{600K}$ (kJ mol$^{-1}$)</th><th style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">$S^\circ_{600K}$ (J mol$^{-1}$ K$^{-1}$)</th></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">$XY(g)$</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">$42$</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">$200$</td></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">$X_2(g)$</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">$8$</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">$140$</td></tr><tr><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">$Y_2(g)$</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">$80$</td><td style="border: 1px solid black; padding: 5px;">$250$</td></tr></table>

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-21000$
- **(B)** $-10$
- **(C)** $-1000$
- **(D)** $-9.012$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $-10$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The given reaction is $X_2(g) + Y_2(g) \rightleftharpoons 2XY(g)$.

The standard enthalpy of reaction, $\Delta_r H^\circ$, is calculated as:
$\Delta_r H^\circ = \sum \Delta_f H^\circ(\text{products}) - \sum \Delta_f H^\circ(\text{reactants})$
$\Delta_r H^\circ = 2 \Delta_f H^\circ(XY) - [\Delta_f H^\circ(X_2) + \Delta_f H^\circ(Y_2)]$
$\Delta_r H^\circ = 2(42) - (8 + 80) = 84 - 88 = -4 \text{ kJ mol}^{-1}$

The standard entropy of reaction, $\Delta_r S^\circ$, is calculated as:
$\Delta_r S^\circ = \sum S^\circ(\text{products}) - \sum S^\circ(\text{reactants})$
$\Delta_r S^\circ = 2 S^\circ(XY) - [S^\circ(X_2) + S^\circ(Y_2)]$
$\Delta_r S^\circ = 2(200) - (140 + 250) = 400 - 390 = 10 \text{ J K}^{-1} \text{ mol}^{-1}$

Converting $\Delta_r S^\circ$ to $\text{kJ K}^{-1} \text{ mol}^{-1}$:
$\Delta_r S^\circ = \dfrac{10}{1000} = 0.01 \text{ kJ K}^{-1} \text{ mol}^{-1}$

Now, using the Gibbs free energy equation:
$\Delta_r G^\circ = \Delta_r H^\circ - T \Delta_r S^\circ$
$\Delta_r G^\circ = -4 - 600 \times 0.01$
$\Delta_r G^\circ = -4 - 6 = -10 \text{ kJ mol}^{-1}$

Answer: $-10$

</details>

### Q3. JEE Main 2026 (04 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

If $3.365$ g of ethanol $(l)$ is burnt completely in a bomb calorimeter at $298.15$ K, the heat produced is $99.472$ kJ. The $|\Delta H_f°|$ of ethanol at $298.15$ K is ______ $\times 10^2$ kJ mol$^{-1}$. (Nearest integer)
Given: Standard enthalpy for combustion of graphite $=-393.5$ kJ mol$^{-1}$
Standard enthalpy of formation of water $(l)=-285.8$ kJ mol$^{-1}$
Molar mass in g mol$^{-1}$ of C, H, O are $12$, $1$ and $16$ respectively

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3**

**Detailed Solution:**

Molar mass of ethanol ($C_2H_5OH$) $= 2(12) + 6(1) + 16 = 46$ g mol$^{-1}$

Number of moles of ethanol burnt, $n = \dfrac{3.365}{46} = 0.07315$ mol

Since the combustion occurs in a bomb calorimeter (constant volume), the heat produced corresponds to the change in internal energy ($\Delta U_c^\circ$).
$\Delta U_c^\circ = -\dfrac{99.472}{0.07315} = -1359.8$ kJ mol$^{-1}$

The balanced chemical equation for the combustion of ethanol is:
$C_2H_5OH(l) + 3O_2(g) \rightarrow 2CO_2(g) + 3H_2O(l)$

Change in the number of gaseous moles, $\Delta n_g = 2 - 3 = -1$

Now, calculating the standard enthalpy of combustion ($\Delta H_c^\circ$):
$\Delta H_c^\circ = \Delta U_c^\circ + \Delta n_g RT$
$\Delta H_c^\circ = -1359.8 + (-1) \times (8.314 \times 10^{-3} \text{ kJ K}^{-1} \text{ mol}^{-1}) \times 298.15 \text{ K}$
$\Delta H_c^\circ = -1359.8 - 2.48 = -1362.28$ kJ mol$^{-1}$

The standard enthalpy of combustion can also be written in terms of enthalpies of formation:
$\Delta H_c^\circ = \sum \Delta H_f^\circ(\text{products}) - \sum \Delta H_f^\circ(\text{reactants})$
$\Delta H_c^\circ = [2\Delta H_f^\circ(CO_2, g) + 3\Delta H_f^\circ(H_2O, l)] - [\Delta H_f^\circ(C_2H_5OH, l) + 3\Delta H_f^\circ(O_2, g)]$

Substituting the given values (note that $\Delta H_f^\circ$ of $O_2(g)$ is $0$):
$-1362.28 = [2(-393.5) + 3(-285.8)] - \Delta H_f^\circ(C_2H_5OH, l)$
$-1362.28 = [-787.0 - 857.4] - \Delta H_f^\circ(C_2H_5OH, l)$
$-1362.28 = -1644.4 - \Delta H_f^\circ(C_2H_5OH, l)$

$\Delta H_f^\circ(C_2H_5OH, l) = -1644.4 + 1362.28 = -282.12$ kJ mol$^{-1}$

The magnitude is $|\Delta H_f^\circ| = 282.12$ kJ mol$^{-1} = 2.8212 \times 10^2$ kJ mol$^{-1}$

Rounding to the nearest integer, we get $3$.

Answer: $3$

</details>

### Q4. JEE Main 2026 (02 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Consider the following data.
(i) $2Al(s) + 6HCl(aq) \rightarrow Al_2Cl_6(aq) + 3H_2(g) + 1200$ kJ/mol
(ii) $H_2(g) + Cl_2(g) \rightarrow 2HCl(g) + 164$ kJ/mol
(iii) $HCl(g) + aq \rightarrow HCl(aq) + 83$ kJ/mol
(iv) $Al_2Cl_6(s) + aq \rightarrow Al_2Cl_6(aq) + 663$ kJ/mol
The enthalpy of formation of anhydrous solid $Al_2Cl_6$ is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-648$ kJ mol$^{-1}$
- **(B)** $-1350$ kJ mol$^{-1}$
- **(C)** $-2002$ kJ mol$^{-1}$
- **(D)** $-1527$ kJ mol$^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $-1527$ kJ mol$^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The given reactions with their enthalpy changes are:
(i) $2Al(s) + 6HCl(aq) \rightarrow Al_2Cl_6(aq) + 3H_2(g)$, $\Delta H_1 = -1200$ kJ/mol
(ii) $H_2(g) + Cl_2(g) \rightarrow 2HCl(g)$, $\Delta H_2 = -164$ kJ/mol
(iii) $HCl(g) + aq \rightarrow HCl(aq)$, $\Delta H_3 = -83$ kJ/mol
(iv) $Al_2Cl_6(s) + aq \rightarrow Al_2Cl_6(aq)$, $\Delta H_4 = -663$ kJ/mol

The required reaction for the enthalpy of formation of anhydrous solid $Al_2Cl_6$ is:
$2Al(s) + 3Cl_2(g) \rightarrow Al_2Cl_6(s)$

This target equation can be obtained by applying the following algebraic operations on the given equations:
Equation (i) $+ 3 \times$ Equation (ii) $+ 6 \times$ Equation (iii) $-$ Equation (iv)

Let us verify by adding the modified equations:
$2Al(s) + 6HCl(aq) \rightarrow Al_2Cl_6(aq) + 3H_2(g)$
$3H_2(g) + 3Cl_2(g) \rightarrow 6HCl(g)$
$6HCl(g) + aq \rightarrow 6HCl(aq)$
$Al_2Cl_6(aq) \rightarrow Al_2Cl_6(s) + aq$

Summing the above reactions and canceling common terms on both sides, we get:
$2Al(s) + 3Cl_2(g) \rightarrow Al_2Cl_6(s)$

The enthalpy of formation is calculated as:
$\Delta H_f = \Delta H_1 + 3\Delta H_2 + 6\Delta H_3 - \Delta H_4$
$\Delta H_f = -1200 + 3(-164) + 6(-83) - (-663)$
$\Delta H_f = -1200 - 492 - 498 + 663$
$\Delta H_f = -2190 + 663 = -1527$ kJ/mol

Answer: $-1527$ kJ mol$^{-1}$

</details>

### Q5. JEE Main 2026 (24 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The heat of atomisation of methane and ethane are ' x ' $\mathrm{kJ} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$ and ' y ' $\mathrm{kJ} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$ respectively. The longest wavelength $(\lambda)$ of light capable of breaking the $\mathrm{C}-\mathrm{C}$ bond can be expressed in SI unit as:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{\mathrm{N}_{\mathrm{A}} \mathrm{hc}}{250(4 y-6 x)}$
- **(B)** $\frac{\mathrm{hc}}{1000}\left(\frac{y-6 x}{4}\right)^{-1}$
- **(C)** $\frac{\mathrm{N}_{\mathrm{A}} \mathrm{hc}}{250(y-6 x)}$
- **(D)** $\mathrm{N}_{\text {A }} \mathrm{hc}\left(y-\frac{6 x}{4}\right)^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\frac{\mathrm{N}_{\mathrm{A}} \mathrm{hc}}{250(4 y-6 x)}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Heat of atomization: CH₄ has x kJ/mol (4 C-H bonds), C₂H₆ has y kJ/mol (1 C-C + 6 C-H bonds)
Let C-H bond energy = a, C-C bond energy = b
Then: x = 4a and y = b + 6a
Solving: a = x/4, so b = y - 6x/4 = (4y - 6x)/4 kJ/mol
Converting to J/mol: b = (4y - 6x) × 250 J/mol
Using $E = \frac{hc}{\lambda}$ and $\lambda = \frac{hc}{E}$:
$\lambda = \frac{N_A hc}{250(4y - 6x)}$ (in SI units)

</details>

### Q6. JEE Main 2026 (24 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

$\mathrm{A} \rightarrow \mathrm{D}$ is an endothermic reaction occurring in three steps (elementary). 
 (i) $\mathrm{A} \rightarrow \mathrm{B} \Delta \mathrm{H}_{i}=+\mathrm{ve}$ 
 (ii) $\mathrm{B} \rightarrow \mathrm{C} \Delta \mathrm{H}_{i i}=-\mathrm{ve}$ 
 (iii) $\mathrm{C} \rightarrow \mathrm{D} \Delta \mathrm{H}_{i i i}=-\mathrm{ve}$ 
 Which of the following graphs between potential energy ($y$-axis) vs reaction coordinate (x -axis) correctly represents the reaction profile of $\mathrm{A} \rightarrow \mathrm{D}$ ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img style="width:150px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/24S1_o_55_1_v2.png">
- **(B)** <img style="width:150px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/24S1_o_55_2_v2.png">
- **(C)** <img style="width:150px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/24S1_o_55_3_v2.png">
- **(D)** <img style="width:150px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/24S1_o_55_4_v2.png">

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <img style="width:150px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/24S1_o_55_1_v2.png">**

**Detailed Solution:**

The reaction A → D occurs through three elementary steps with energy changes: 
Step (i): A → B is endothermic ($\Delta H_I = +ve$), so B has higher energy than A, creating the first peak. 
Step (ii): B → C is exothermic ($\Delta H_{II} = -ve$), so C has lower energy than B, creating a lower second peak.
Step (iii): C → D is exothermic ($\Delta H_{III} = -ve$), so D has lower energy than C, creating an even lower third feature. 
Overall reaction: Since step (i) is endothermic and steps (ii) and (iii) are exothermic, the reaction can be endothermic overall depending on the magnitudes. The overall energy change is positive (A → D is endothermic), meaning D must be at higher energy than A. 
The reaction profile must show three distinct peaks/steps with the final product D positioned higher than the initial reactant A.
 (1) correctly depicts this three-step mechanism with proper energy relationships and overall endothermic character.

</details>

### Q7. JEE Main 2026 (22 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

If the enthalpy of sublimation of Li is $155 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$, enthalpy of dissociation of $\mathrm{F}_{2}$ is $150 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$, ionization enthalpy of Li is $520 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$, electron gain enthalpy of F is $-313 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$, standard enthalpy of formation of LiF is $-594 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$. The magnitude of lattice enthalpy of LiF is $\_\_\_\_$ $\mathrm{kJ} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$. (Nearest Integer)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1031**

**Detailed Solution:**

Using Born-Haber cycle:
$\Delta H_f(LiF) = \Delta H_{sub}(Li) + \frac{1}{2}\Delta H_{diss}(F_2) + \Delta H_{ie}(Li) + \Delta H_{eg}(F) - U_{lattice}$

Substituting values: $-594 = 155 + \frac{1}{2}(150) + 520 + (-313) - U_{lattice}$

$-594 = 155 + 75 + 520 - 313 - U_{lattice}$

$-594 = 437 - U_{lattice}$

$U_{lattice} = 437 + 594 = 1031$ kJ/mol

</details>

### Q8. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Consider the following data: 
 $\Delta_{f} \mathrm{H}^{\ominus}$ (methane, g$)=-\mathrm{X} \mathrm{kJ} \mathrm{mol}{ }^{-1}$ 
 Enthalpy of sublimation of graphite $=\mathrm{Y} \mathrm{kJ} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$ 
 Dissociation enthalpy of $\mathrm{H}_{2}=\mathrm{Z} \mathrm{kJ} \mathrm{mol}{ }^{-1}$ 
 The bond enthalpy of $\mathrm{C}-\mathrm{H}$ bond is given by :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{-X+Y+Z}{4}$
- **(B)** $\frac{X+Y+4 Z}{2}$
- **(C)** $\frac{X+Y+2 Z}{4}$
- **(D)** $X+Y+Z$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $\frac{X+Y+2 Z}{4}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For methane formation: $\text{C(g)} + 2\text{H}_2(g) \rightarrow \text{CH}_4(g)$. 
Using enthalpy balance: $\Delta_f H° = \text{Bonds broken} - \text{Bonds formed}$. 
Breaking: sublimation of C (Y) and 2 H-H bonds (2Z); Forming: 4 C-H bonds. 
Energy equation: $-X = Y + 2Z - 4B$ where B is C-H bond enthalpy. 
Solving: $4B = X + Y + 2Z$, so $B = \frac{X + Y + 2Z}{4}$.

</details>

The hydration energies of $\mathrm{K}^{+}$and $\mathrm{Cl}^{-}$are -x and -y $\mathrm{kJ} / \mathrm{mol}$ respectively. If lattice energy of KCl is -z $\mathrm{kJ} / \mathrm{mol}$, then the heat of solution of KCl is :

- **(B)** $x+y+z$
- **(C)** $z-(x+y)$

$\begin{aligned} \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\text {Sol}^{\mathrm{n}}.} & =\mathrm{L} \cdot \mathrm{E} \cdot+(\mathrm{H} \cdot \mathrm{E})_{\mathrm{K}_{(\mathrm{g})}^{+}}+(\mathrm{HE})_{\mathrm{Cl}_{(\mathrm{g})}^{-1}} \\ & =\mathrm{Z}-\mathrm{x}-\mathrm{y} \\ & =\mathrm{z}-(\mathrm{x}+\mathrm{y})\end{aligned}$

### Q12. JEE Main 2025 (7 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Total enthalpy change for freezing of 1 mol of water at $10^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ to ice at $-10^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ is ____
(Given : $\Delta_{\text {fus }} H=x \mathrm{~kJ} / \mathrm{mol}$)
$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{p}}\left[\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\mathrm{l})\right]=\mathrm{y} \mathrm{J} \mathrm{mol}^{-1} \mathrm{~K}^{-1} \\ & \mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{p}}\left[\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\mathrm{s})\right]=\mathrm{z} \mathrm{J} \mathrm{mol}^{-1} \mathrm{~K}^{-1}\end{aligned}$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-x-10 y-10 z$
- **(B)** $-10(100 x+y+z)$
- **(C)** $10(100 x+y+z)$
- **(D)** $x-10 y-10 z$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $-10(100 x+y+z)$**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/Lc_0owLQrGhWe2AqR-xJrL33uFCDtWYPIBFq3PKpvwc.original.fullsize.png"/>
$\begin{aligned} & \Delta H=1 \times y(0-10)-x \times 1000+1 \times z\left(-10^{\circ}-0^{\circ}\right) \\ & \Delta H=-10(100 x+y+z) \text { Joule. }\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q13. JEE Main 2025 (4 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Consider the given data :
(a) $\mathrm{HCl}(\mathrm{g})+10 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\mathrm{l}) \rightarrow \mathrm{HCl}.10 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}$
$\Delta \mathrm{H}=-69.01 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
(b) $\mathrm{HCl}(\mathrm{g})+40 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\mathrm{l}) \rightarrow \mathrm{HCl}.40 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}$
$\Delta \mathrm{H}=-72.79 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
Choose the correct statement :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Dissolution of gas in water is an endothermic process
- **(B)** The heat of solution depends on the amount of solvent.
- **(C)** The heat of dilution for the $\mathrm{HCl}\left(\mathrm{HCl} \cdot 10 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right.$ to $\left.\mathrm{HCl}.40 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}\right)$ is $3.78 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$.
- **(D)** The heat of formation of HCl solution is represented by both (a) and (b)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) The heat of solution depends on the amount of solvent.**

**Detailed Solution:**

From the given information
$\Delta \mathrm{H}$ is negative so it means dissolution of gas $\mathrm{HCl}(\mathrm{g})$ is exothermic.
$\mathrm{HCl}(\mathrm{g})+10 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\mathrm{l}) \rightarrow \mathrm{HCl}.10 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}$  ...(1)
$\Delta \mathrm{H}_1=-69.01 \frac{\mathrm{~kJ}}{\mathrm{~mol}}$
$\mathrm{HCl}(\mathrm{g})+40 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\mathrm{l}) \rightarrow \mathrm{HCl}.40 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}$ ...(2)
$\Delta \mathrm{H}_2=-72.79 \frac{\mathrm{~kJ}}{\mathrm{~mol}}$
Hence heat of solution depends upon amount of solvent
By equation..(2) - equation
$\mathrm{HCl}.10 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}+30 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\ell) \rightarrow \mathrm{HCl} \cdot 40 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}$
So Heat of dilution $=-72.79-(-69.01)$
$=-3.78 \frac{\mathrm{~kJ}}{\mathrm{~mol}}$
Hence option (3) is incorrect.
For heat of formation reactant should be in elemental form hence option (4) is incorrect

</details>

### Q14. JEE Main 2025 (3 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

A sample of n-octane $(1.14 \mathrm{~g})$ was completely burnt in excess of oxygen in a bomb calorimeter, whose heat capacity is $5 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~K}^{-1}$. As a result of combustion reaction, the temperature of the calorimeter is increased by 5 K. The magnitude of the heat of combustion of octane at constant volume is ______ $\mathrm{kJ} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$ (nearest integer).

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2500**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\text { Mole of octane }=\frac{1.14}{114}=0.01 \mathrm{~mole}$
Heat evolved $=\mathrm{C} \times \Delta \mathrm{T}$
$\begin{aligned}
& =5 \times 5 \mathrm{~kJ} \\
& =25 \mathrm{~kJ}
\end{aligned}$
$\therefore$ Magnitude of Heat of combustion $=\frac{25}{0.01}=2500$ $\mathrm{kJ} /$ mole

</details>

### Q15. JEE Main 2025 (3 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given below are two statements :
Statement I : When a system containing ice in equilibrium with water (liquid) is heated, heat is absorbed by the system and there is no change in the temperature of the system until whole ice gets melted.
Statement II : At melting point of ice, there is absorption of heat in order to overcome intermolecular forces of attraction within the molecules of water in ice and kinetic energy of molecules is not increased at melting point.
In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below:

**Options:**

- **(A)** Statement I is true but Statement II is false
- **(B)** Both Statement I and Statement II are false
- **(C)** Both Statement I and Statement II are true
- **(D)** Statement I is false but Statement II is true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Both Statement I and Statement II are true**

**Detailed Solution:**

At melting point when ice melts, supplied heat is utilised to overcome intermolecular attraction within the molecules so temperature remain constant.

</details>

### Q16. JEE Main 2025 (3 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given :
$\begin{aligned}
& \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\text {sub }}^{\ominus}[\mathrm{C}(\text { graphite })]=710 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \\
& \Delta_{\mathrm{C}-\mathrm{H}} \mathrm{H}^{\ominus}=414 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \\
& \Delta_{\mathrm{H}-\mathrm{H}} \mathrm{H}^{\Theta}=436 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \\
& \Delta_{\mathrm{C}=\mathrm{C}} \mathrm{H}^{\Theta}=611 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}
\end{aligned}$
The $\Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\ominus}$ for $\mathrm{CH}_2=\mathrm{CH}_2$ is _____ $\mathrm{kJ} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$ (nearest integer value)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **25**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/4B7d00XLNFLMv1GaV2IuXh9ZHrboQtGl_JVSRK7ofJk.original.fullsize.png"/>
$\begin{aligned} & {\left[\Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\mathrm{o}}\right]_{\mathrm{C}_2 \mathrm{H}_{(4)}}=2 \times\left[\Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{sub}}^{\mathrm{o}}\right]_{\mathrm{C}_{(\mathrm{s})}}+2 \times \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{H}-\mathrm{H}}^{\mathrm{o}}-1 \times \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{C}=\mathrm{C}}^{\mathrm{o}}-4 \times \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{CH}}^{\mathrm{o}} } \\ \Rightarrow \quad & {\left[\Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\mathrm{o}}\right]_{\mathrm{C}_2 \mathrm{H}_{4(\mathrm{~g})}}=(2 \times 710)+(2 \times 436)-611-4 \times 414 } \\ \Rightarrow \quad & {\left[\Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\mathrm{o}}\right]_{\mathrm{C}_2 \mathrm{H}_{4(\mathrm{~g})}}=25 \mathrm{~kJ} / \mathrm{mol} }\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q18. JEE Main 2025 (28 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Consider the following data :
Heat of formation of $\mathrm{CO}_2(\mathrm{~g})=-393.5 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
Heat of formation of $\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\mathrm{l})=-286.0 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
Heat of combustion of benzene $=-3267.0 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
The heat of formation of benzene is _ $\mathrm{kJ} \mathrm{mol}{ }^{-1}$. (Nearest integer)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **48**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{f}}\left[\mathrm{CO}_2(\mathrm{~g})\right]=-393.5 \mathrm{~kJ} / \mathrm{mole} \\ & \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{f}}\left[\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\ell)\right]=-286.0 \mathrm{~kJ} / \mathrm{mole} \\ & \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{c}}\left[\mathrm{C}_6 \mathrm{H}_6\right]=-3267.0 \mathrm{~kJ} / \mathrm{mole} \\ & \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{f}} \mathrm{C}_6 \mathrm{H}_6=(?) \\ & \mathrm{C}_6 \mathrm{H}_6+\frac{15}{2} \mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \longrightarrow 6 \mathrm{CO}_2(\mathrm{~g})+3 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\ell) \\ & \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{R}}=\Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{C}}=\Sigma \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathrm{P})-\Sigma \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathrm{R}) \\ & -3267=6 \times(-393.5)+3(-286)-\Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{f}}\left(\mathrm{C}_6 \mathrm{H}_6\right) \\ & \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{f}}\left(\mathrm{C}_6 \mathrm{H}_6\right)=48 \mathrm{~kJ} / \mathrm{mole}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q19. JEE Main 2025 (28 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The formation enthalpies, $\Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{f}} \ominus$ for $\mathrm{H}_{(\mathrm{g})}$ and $\mathrm{O}_{(\mathrm{g})}$ are 220.0 and $250.0 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$, respectively, at 298.15 K , and $\Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{f}}{ }^{\ominus}$ for $\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}_{(\mathrm{g})}$ is $-242.0 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ at the same temperature. The average bond enthalpy of the $\mathrm{O}-\mathrm{H}$ bond in water at 298.15 K is __________ $\mathrm{kJ} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$ (nearest integer).

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **466**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \frac{1}{2} \mathrm{H}_{2(\mathrm{~g})} \rightarrow \mathrm{H}(\mathrm{g}) \quad ; \quad \Delta_{\mathrm{f}} \mathrm{H}\left(\mathrm{H}_{(\mathrm{g})}\right)=220 \mathrm{KJ} / \mathrm{mol} \\ & \frac{1}{2} \mathrm{O}_{2(\mathrm{~g})} \rightarrow \mathrm{O}(\mathrm{g}) \quad ; \quad \Delta_{\mathrm{f}} \mathrm{H}\left(\mathrm{O}_{(\mathrm{g})}\right)=250 \mathrm{KJ} / \mathrm{mol}\end{aligned}$

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/OoxJZiNSpHhqGQkYYkGlloL0zjAz8Xhvijibxy1uH-s.original.fullsize.png">


$\begin{aligned} & \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{f}}\left(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}_{(\mathrm{l})}\right)=-242=440+250-2(\mathrm{~B} . \mathrm{E} .(\mathrm{O}-\mathrm{H})) \\ & \mathrm{BE}(\mathrm{O}-\mathrm{H})=466 \mathrm{KJ} / \mathrm{mol}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q20. JEE Main 2025 (24 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{S}(\mathrm{~g})+\frac{3}{2} \mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{SO}_3(\mathrm{~g})+2 x \mathrm{kcal} \\
& \mathrm{SO}_2(\mathrm{~g})+\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{SO}_3(\mathrm{~g})+y \mathrm{kcal}
\end{aligned}$

The heat of formation of $\mathrm{SO}_2(\mathrm{~g})$ is given by :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $x+y \mathrm{kcal}$
- **(B)** $y-2 x \mathrm{kcal}$
- **(C)** $\frac{2 x}{y} \mathrm{kcal}$
- **(D)** $2 x+y \mathrm{kcal}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $y-2 x \mathrm{kcal}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

(i) $\mathrm{S}(\mathrm{g})+\frac{3}{2} \mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{SO}_3(\mathrm{~g})+2 x$ kcal $\Delta \mathrm{H}_1$
(ii) $\mathrm{SO}_2(\mathrm{~g})+\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{SO}_3(\mathrm{~g})+y$ kcal $\Delta \mathrm{H}_2$
(i) - (ii)
$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{S}(\mathrm{~g})+\mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{SO}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \Delta \mathrm{H} \\
& \Delta \mathrm{H}=\Delta \mathrm{H}_1-\Delta \mathrm{H}_2 \\
& =-2 x-(-y)=(y-2 x) \mathrm{kcal}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q21. JEE Main 2025 (23 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The bond dissociation enthalpy of $\mathrm{X}_2 \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\text {bond }}$ calculated from the given data is $\qquad$ $\mathrm{kJ} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$. (Nearest integer)
$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{M}^{+} \mathrm{X}^{-}(\mathrm{s}) \rightarrow \mathrm{M}^{+}(\mathrm{g})+\mathrm{X}^{-}(\mathrm{g}) \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\text {lattice }}^*=800 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \\
& \mathrm{M}(\mathrm{~s}) \rightarrow \mathrm{M}(\mathrm{~g}) \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\text {sub }}^{\circ}=100 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}
\end{aligned}$
$\mathrm{M}(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{M}^{+}(\mathrm{g})+\mathrm{e}^{-}(\mathrm{g}) \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{i}}=500 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
$\mathrm{X}(\mathrm{~g})+\mathrm{e}^{-}(\mathrm{g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{X}^{-}(\mathrm{g}) \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{eg}}^*=-300 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
$\mathrm{M}(\mathrm{~s})+\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{X}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{M}^{+} \mathrm{X}^{-}(\mathrm{s}) \Delta \mathrm{H}_f^{\circ}=-400 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
[Given : $\mathrm{M}^{+} \mathrm{X}^{-}$is a pure ionic compound and X forms a diatomic molecule $\mathrm{X}_2$ in gaseous state]

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **200**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/1Om-NhkKvGPDQJQU6kLYSo9xpzB-zIs-foAUgmIb8RA.original.fullsize.png">


$\begin{aligned} & \begin{array}{l}\therefore \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{f}}(\mathrm{MX})= \\ \quad \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\text {sub }}(\mathrm{M})+\text { I.E. }(\mathrm{M})+\frac{1}{2}[\text { B.E. }(\mathrm{X}-\mathrm{X})] \\ \\ +\mathrm{EG}(\mathrm{X})+\text { L.E. }(\mathrm{MX})\end{array} \\ & -400=(100)+(500)+\frac{1}{2}(\text { B.E. })+(-300)+(-800)\end{aligned} \quad \begin{aligned} & \therefore \text { B.E. }=200 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mole}^{-1}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q23. JEE Main 2025 (22 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Consider the following cases of standard enthalpy of reaction $\left(\Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{r}}^{\circ}\right.$ in $\left.\mathrm{kJ} \mathrm{mol}{ }^{-1}\right)$
$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{C}_2 \mathrm{H}_6(\mathrm{~g})+\frac{7}{2} \mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow 2 \mathrm{CO}_2(\mathrm{~g})+3 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\mathrm{l}) \Delta \mathrm{H}_1^{\circ}=-1550 \\
& \mathrm{C} \text { (graphite) }+\mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{CO}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \quad \Delta \mathrm{H}_2^{\circ}=-393.5 \\
& \mathrm{H}_2(\mathrm{~g})+\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\mathrm{l}) \quad \Delta \mathrm{H}_3^{\circ}=-286
\end{aligned}$

The magnitude of $\Delta \mathrm{H}_{f \mathrm{C}_2 \mathrm{H}_6(\mathrm{~g})}^{\circ}$ is _______ $\mathrm{kJ} \mathrm{mol}{ }^{-1}$ (Nearest integer).

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **95**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & 2 \mathrm{C}_{\text {(graphite) }}+3 \mathrm{H}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{C}_2 \mathrm{H}_6(\mathrm{~g}) \quad \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{f}}=? \\ & \mathrm{C}_2 \mathrm{H}_6(\mathrm{~g})+\frac{7}{2} \mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow 2 \mathrm{CO}_2(\mathrm{~g})+3 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(l) \\ & \quad \Delta \mathrm{H}_1=-1550 \\ & \mathrm{C}_{\text {(graphite) }}+\mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{CO}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \Delta \mathrm{H}_2=-393.5 \\ & \mathrm{H}_2(\mathrm{~g})+\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(/) \Delta \mathrm{H}_3=-286 \\ & \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{f}}=2 \Delta \mathrm{H}_2+3 \Delta \mathrm{H}_3-\Delta \mathrm{H}_1 \\ & \quad=95 \mathrm{~kJ} / \mathrm{mole}^2\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q25. JEE Main 2024 (09 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

When equal volume of \(1 \mathrm{M} \mathrm{HCl}\) and \(1 \mathrm{M} \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{SO}_4\) are separately neutralised by excess volume of \(1 \mathrm{M}\) \(\mathrm{NaOH}\) solution. \(x\) and \(y \mathrm{~kJ}\) of heat is liberated respectively. The value of \(y / x\) is _______

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{H}^{+}+\mathrm{OH}^{-} \rightarrow \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O} \Rightarrow \mathrm{x} \\ & 2 \mathrm{H}^{+}+2 \mathrm{OH}^{-} \rightarrow 2 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O} \Rightarrow 2 \mathrm{x}=\mathrm{y} \\ & \mathrm{y} / \mathrm{x}=2\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q26. JEE Main 2024 (05 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Combustion of 1 mole of benzene is expressed at
\(\mathrm{C}_6 \mathrm{H}_6(\mathrm{l})+\frac{15}{2} \mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow 6 \mathrm{CO}_2(\mathrm{~g})+3 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\mathrm{l}) \text {. }\)
The standard enthalpy of combustion of \(2 \mathrm{~mol}\) of benzene is \(-^{\prime} x^{\prime} \mathrm{kJ}\).
\(x=\) ______
Given:
1. standard Enthalpy of formation of \(1 \mathrm{~mol}\) of \(\mathrm{C}_6 \mathrm{H}_6(\mathrm{l})\), for the reaction \(6 \mathrm{C}\) (graphite) \(+3 \mathrm{H}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{C}_6 \mathrm{H}_6(\mathrm{l})\) is \(48.5 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\).
2. Standard Enthalpy of formation of \(1 \mathrm{~mol}\) of \(\mathrm{CO}_2(\mathrm{~g})\), for the reaction \(\mathrm{C}\) (graphite) \(+\mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{CO}_2(\mathrm{~g})\) is \(-393.5 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\).
3. Standard and Enthalpy of formation of \(1 \mathrm{~mol}\) of \(\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\mathrm{l})\), for the reaction \(\mathrm{H}_2(\mathrm{~g})+\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\mathrm{l})\) is \(-286 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\).

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **6535**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & 6 \mathrm{C}(\text { graphite })+3 \mathrm{H}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{C}_6 \mathrm{H}_6(\ell) ; \Delta \mathrm{H}=48.5 \mathrm{~kJ} / \mathrm{mol} \\ & \mathrm{C}(\text { graphite })+\mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{CO}_2(\mathrm{~g}) ; \Delta \mathrm{H}=-393.5 \mathrm{~kJ} / \mathrm{mol} \\ & \mathrm{H}_2^{(\mathrm{g})}+\frac{1}{2}(\mathrm{~g}) \longrightarrow \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\ell) ; \Delta \mathrm{H}=-286 \mathrm{~kJ} / \mathrm{mol} \\ & \text { equation }-(1) \times 1+(2) \times 6+(3) \times 3 \\ & -48.5-6 \times 393.5-3 \times 286 \\ & =-3267.5 \mathrm{~kJ} \text { for } 1 \mathrm{~mol} \\ & =-6535 \mathrm{~kJ} \text { for } 2 \mathrm{~mol} \\ & \text { Ans. } 6535 \mathrm{~kJ}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q27. JEE Main 2024 (05 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The heat of combustion of solid benzoic acid at constant volume is \(-321.30 \mathrm{~kJ}\) at \(27^{\circ} \mathrm{C}\). The heat of combustion at constant pressure is \((-321.30-x \mathrm{R}) \mathrm{kJ}\), the value of \(x\) is _______.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **150**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{C}_6 \mathrm{H}_5 \mathrm{COOH}(\mathrm{S})+\frac{15}{2} \mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow 7 \mathrm{CO}_2(\mathrm{~g})+3 \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\ell) \\ & \Delta \mathrm{H}=\Delta \mathrm{U}+\Delta \mathrm{n}_{\mathrm{g}} \mathrm{RT} \\ & =-321.30-\frac{1}{2} \frac{\mathrm{R}}{100} \times 300 \\ & =(-321.30-150 \mathrm{R}) \mathrm{kJ}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q28. JEE Main 2024 (05 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Given below are two statements : One is labelled as Assertion (A) and the other is labelled as Reason (R)
Assertion (A) : Enthalpy of neutralisation of strong monobasic acid with strong monoacidic base is always \(-57 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\).
Reason (R) : Enthalpy of neutralisation is the amount of heat liberated when one mole of \(\mathrm{H}^{+}\) ions furnished by acid combine with one mole of \(\mathrm{OH}^{-}\) ions furnished by base to form one mole of water.
In the light of the above statements, choose the correct answer from the options given below.

**Options:**

- **(A)** (A) is true but (R) is false
- **(B)** Both (A) and (R) are true but (R) is not the correct explanation of (A)
- **(C)** Both (A) and (R) are true and (R) is the correct explanation of (A)
- **(D)** (A) is false but (R) is true

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) Both (A) and (R) are true and (R) is the correct explanation of (A)**

**Detailed Solution:**

Enthalpy of neutralization of SA & SB is always $-57 \mathrm{~kJ} / \mathrm{mol}$ because strong monoacid gives one mole of $\mathrm{H}^{+}$ and strong mono base gives one mole of $\mathrm{OH}^{-}$ which form one mole of water.

</details>

### Q29. JEE Main 2024 (04 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The enthalpy of formation of ethane \(\left(\mathrm{C}_2 \mathrm{H}_6\right)\) from ethylene by addition of hydrogen where the bond-energies of \(\mathrm{C}-\mathrm{H}, \mathrm{C}-\mathrm{C}, \mathrm{C}=\mathrm{C}, \mathrm{H}-\mathrm{H}\) are \(414 \mathrm{~kJ}, 347 \mathrm{~kJ}, 615 \mathrm{~kJ}\) and \(435 \mathrm{~kJ}\) respectively is \(\qquad\) \(\mathrm{kJ}\)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **125**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{C}_2 \mathrm{H}_4(\mathrm{~g})+\mathrm{H}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{C}_2 \mathrm{H}_6(\mathrm{~g}) \\ & \Delta \mathrm{H}=\mathrm{BE}(\mathrm{C}=\mathrm{C})+4 \mathrm{BE}(\mathrm{C}-\mathrm{H})+\mathrm{BE}(\mathrm{H}-\mathrm{H}) \\ & -\mathrm{BE}(\mathrm{C}-\mathrm{C})-6 \mathrm{BE}(\mathrm{C}-\mathrm{H}) \\ & \Delta \mathrm{H}=\mathrm{BE}(\mathrm{C}=\mathrm{C})+\mathrm{BE}(\mathrm{H}-\mathrm{H})-\mathrm{BE}(\mathrm{C}-\mathrm{C}) \\ & -2 \mathrm{BE}(\mathrm{C}-\mathrm{H}) \\ & =615+435-347-2 \times 414 \\ & =-125 \mathrm{~kJ}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q30. JEE Main 2024 (30 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Two reactions are given below:
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>(</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>→</mo><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>(</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><mi>Δ</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>822</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>(</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>→</mo><msub><mi>CO</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><mi>Δ</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>110</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math></p><p>Then enthalpy change for following reaction
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>(</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>→</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>CO</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **492**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Enthalpy change is the standard enthalpy of formation, in any general chemical reaction, the reactants undergo chemical changes and combine to give products. It can be represented by the following equation:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Reactant</mi><mo>→</mo><mi>Product</mi></math></p><p>From the given equations,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>(</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>→</mo><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>(</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><mi>Δ</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>822</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math> .........(i)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>(</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>→</mo><msub><mi>CO</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><mi>ΔH</mi><mo>°</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>110</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math>....(ii)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>(</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>→</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mi>CO</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>ΔH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>?</mo></math></p><p>The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>ΔH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> can be calculated as,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>ΔH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>×</mo><msub><mi>ΔH</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>ΔH</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>110</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>822</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>492</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mole</mi></math></p></blockquote>

### Q31. JEE Main 2024 (29 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Standard enthalpy of vapourisation for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>30</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. Heat required for vapourisation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>284</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math> at constant temperature is __________ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>kJ</mi></math>. (Given molar mass in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>gmol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>12</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>Cl</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>35</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn></math>)</p><p>Round off your answer to the nearest integer.</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **56**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The enthalpy of vapourisation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mo>∆</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>∆</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math></p><p>No. of moles of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>given</mi><mo> </mo><mi>mass</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>molar</mi><mo> </mo><mi>mass</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>                                   <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>284</mn><mn>154</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>844</mn><mo> </mo><mi>mol</mi></math></p><p>Hence, the heat required for the vapourisation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>844</mn><mo> </mo><mi>mol</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>844</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mi>Heat</mi><mo> </mo><mi>required</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>56</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>242</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi><mo>≈</mo><mn>56</mn><mo> </mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q32. JEE Main 2023 (13 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>AB</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>&#916;</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>200</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AB</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> are diatomic molecules. If the bond enthalpies of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AB</mi></math> are in the ratio<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo></math> then the bond enthalpy of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>_________kJ</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> (Nearest integer)</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **800**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>First write balanced reaction for the formation of one mole of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>AB</mi></math> with the help of diatomic molecule:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>AB</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>&#916;H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>200</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math></p><p>Now, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi>reaction</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow></msub><mo>-</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi>AB</mi></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfenced><mrow><mi>let</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi>AB</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>200</mn><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>200</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>25</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>200</mn><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>800</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math></p><p>Bond enthalpy <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>800</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p>

</details>

### Q33. JEE Main 2023 (11 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>Solid fuel used in rocket is a mixture of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>&#160;</mo></math>and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Al</mi></math> (in ratio <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn></math>). The heat evolved <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>)</mo></math> per gram of the mixture is _____ (Nearest integer)</p><p>Given <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>&#916;H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>Al</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1700</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>&#916;H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>840</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>Molar mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Fe</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>AI</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>56</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>27</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>16</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> respectively</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **4**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Molten iron can be prepared by alumino thermite process as shown below.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>Al</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>Fe</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>Al</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math></p><p>The iron oxide and aluminium metal ratio is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>2</mn></math> in the above reaction.</p><p>Now, the Enthalpy of the reaction is equal to the difference of enthalpy of formation of products to the enthalpy of formation of reactants.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msubsup><mi>&#916;H</mi><mtext>reaction&#160;</mtext><mn>0</mn></msubsup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1700</mn><mo>+</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>840</mn><mo>)</mo></math></p><p> Ratio of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>Fe</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Al</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>160</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>:</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>54</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>96</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>in</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>214</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>gm</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></math>mixture </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#916;H</mi><mo>&#176;</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mi>gm</mi><mtext>&#160;mixture&#160;</mtext></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>860</mn></mrow><mn>214</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>gram</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q34. JEE Main 2023 (10 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

<p>The number of endothermic process/es from the following is _______.</p><p>A. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>B. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HCl</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>Cl</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>C. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>D. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><msub><mi>CO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>E. Dissolution of ammonium chloride in water</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **4**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The decomposition reactions are endothermic reactions. The reactions <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HCl</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>Cl</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></math> are decomposition reactions. The heat vaporisation is an endothermic reaction. Hence, the conversion of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mfenced><mover><mo>&#8594;</mo><mrow></mrow></mover><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced></math> is endothermic process. The heat of combustion is an exothermic process. The heat involved in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#10230;</mo><msub><mi>CO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></math> is heat of combustion. The dissolution of ammonium chloride in water is an endothermic process. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mi>Cl</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><msubsup><mi>NH</mi><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo></msubsup><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>Cl</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo></msup><mo>(</mo><mi>aq</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#916;H</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>.</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1</mn><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p>

</details>

### Q37. JEE Main 2023 (10 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

The enthalpy change for the adsorption process and micelle formation respectively are

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;H</mi><mi>ads</mi></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;H</mi><mi>mic</mi></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;H</mi><mi>ads</mi></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;H</mi><mi>mic</mi></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;H</mi><mi>ads</mi></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;H</mi><mi>mic</mi></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;H</mi><mi>ads</mi></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;H</mi><mi>mic</mi></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;H</mi><mi>ads</mi></msub><mo>&#60;</mo><mn>0</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#916;H</mi><mi>mic</mi></msub><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Adsorption is exothermic process due to decrease in surface energy. Micelle formation is endothermic, it is due to For any spontaneous process entropy should always increase, hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;S</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>0</mn></math> for micelle formation and micelle formation decreases the stability of the colloidal solution so, energy of the mixture should increase which means <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;H</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>.</p><p>The adsorption process <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8211;</mo><mi>ve</mi></math>. For micelle formation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mi>ve</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mi>ve</mi></math>.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q38. JEE Main 2023 (08 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

For complete combustion of ethene,
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>CO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
the amount of heat produced as measured in bomb calorimeter is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1406</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>. The minimum value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>T&#916;S</mi></math> needed to reach equilibrium is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mo>)</mo></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>kJ</mi></math>. (Nearest integer)
Given: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1411**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>CO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;U</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>1406</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>300</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;H</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#916;U</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>&#916;n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></msub><mi>RT</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;U</mi></math> is the change in the internal energy of the system, which is the sum of the kinetic and potential energies of all the molecules in the system. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math> is the gas constant (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>314</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mo>/</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>mol</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mo>)</mo></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math> is the temperature in Kelvin.
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>1406</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>300</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>1406</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8211;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>98</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>1410</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>98</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>KJ</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8776;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>1411</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mi>The</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>minimum</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>value</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>of</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>at</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>equilibrium</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">S</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>1411</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>KJ</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p></blockquote>

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> of ethane undergoes combustion at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>27</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> in a bomb calorimeter. The temperature of calorimeter system (including the water) is found to rise by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>. The heat evolved during combustion of ethane at constant pressure is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>kJmol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>.
(Nearest integer)</p><p>[Given : The heat capacity of the calorimeter system is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi>JK</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>.
Assume ideal gas behaviour.</p><p>Assume ideal gas behaviour.</p><p>Atomic mass of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math> are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> respectively]</p>

xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><msub><mi xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mi>&#916;</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>436</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mi>&#916;</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>0</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>249</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p>What would be the value of X for the following reaction? (Nearest integer)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mi>OH</mi><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>kJmol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **499**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>242</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo><msup><mi>kJmol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> ....(1)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>OH</mi><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>78</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>  ....(2)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>436</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>  ....(3)
Using Hess&#39;s law of constant heat summation, adding equations(1), (2) and (3) and dividing the net result by 2 we get:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>OH</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;&#160;</mo><mn>998</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>499</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
Thus <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>499</mn></math></p><p> </p>

</details>

### Q42. JEE Main 2023 (31 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

Enthalpies of formation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>CO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>HCl</mi></math> are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>105</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>242</mn><mo>,</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>394</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>92</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> respectively. The magnitude of enthalpy of the reaction given below is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>kJmol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. (nearest integer) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#8594;</mo><msub><mi>CO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>HCl</mi><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **173**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Using Hess&#39;s law of constant heat summation:
Enthalpy of reaction = Enthalpy of formation of products - Enthalpy of formation of reactants
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><msub><mi>&#916;H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>&#916;H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>&#176;</mo><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>CO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>&#916;H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>HCl</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>-</mo><msub><mi>&#916;H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>&#176;</mo><mfenced><msub><mi>CCl</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>&#916;H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><mo>&#176;</mo><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>[</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>394</mn><mo>]</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>[</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>92</mn><mo>]</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>105</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>[</mo><mn>242</mn><mo>]</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>394</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>368</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>105</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>484</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>173</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mole</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q44. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

(Enthalpy of neutralisation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>57</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> and Specific heat of water <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>JK</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>) (Neglect heat capacity of flask)

### Q45. JEE Main 2022 (27 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

A gas ( Molar mass <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>280</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>) was burnt in excess <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> in a constant volume calorimeter and during combustion the temperature of calorimeter increased from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>298</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>298</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>45</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math>. If the heat capacity of calorimeter is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> and enthalpy of combustion of gas is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>9</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> then amount of gas burnt is____<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math>.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **35**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Let <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math> is burnt</p><p>Moles<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>280</mn></mfrac></math> heat released by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>280</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mole</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>45</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi></math> heat released by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn></math> mole <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>45</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>280</mn></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mfrac><mi>kJ</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;H</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#916;U</mi><mo>+</mo><mi>&#916;ngRT</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#916;H</mi><mo>&#8771;</mo><mi>&#916;U</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>9</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>280</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>45</mn></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>35</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>
