### Q77. JEE Main 2017 (02 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Given:

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mfenced><mi>graphite</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><msub><mi>CO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>393</mn><mo>.5</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mfenced><mo>;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>285</mn><mo>.8</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>kJ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CO</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup><mo>=</mo><mo>+</mo><mn>890</mn><mo>.3</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>kJ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

Based on the above thermochemical equations, the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup></math> at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>298</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></math> for the reaction

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mi>graphite</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mfenced separators="|"><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced></math> will be:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>144</mn><mo>.0</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>kJ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>74</mn><mo>.8</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>kJ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>144</mn><mo>.0</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>kJ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mn>74</mn><mo>.8</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>kJ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>74</mn><mo>.8</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>kJ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The standard enthalpy of formation is defined as the change in enthalpy when one mole of a substance in the standard state (1 atm of pressure and 298.15 K) is formed from its pure elements under the same conditions.</p><p>The enthalpy of combustion of a substance is defined as the heat energy given out when one mole of a substance burns completely in oxygen.</p><p>The formation reaction of methane is </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mfenced><mi>graphite</mi></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>&#8594;</mo><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced></math></p><p>The heat of combustion data for <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>graphite</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>and</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mo>=</mo><munder><mo>&#8721;</mo><mrow/></munder><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi>Combustion</mi></msub><mfenced><mi>reactants</mi></mfenced><mo>-</mo><munder><mrow><mo>&#8721;</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi>Combustion</mi></msub><mfenced><mi>products</mi></mfenced></mrow><mrow/></munder></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mfenced><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mfenced><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mfenced><mi>graphite</mi></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced></mrow></mfenced><mo>-</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mfenced><mrow><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub><mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><msubsup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msubsup><mfenced><msub><mi>CH</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>393</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>285</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>+</mo><mn>890</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>3</mn></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#916;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">f</mi></msub><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></msup></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>CH</mi><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo></mrow></msub><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">g</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#8722;</mo><mn>74</mn><mo>.8</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math></p></blockquote>

### Q78. JEE Main 2016 (03 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The heats of combustion of carbon and carbon monoxide are <math><mo>-</mo><mn>393.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>283.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo></math> respectively. The heat of formation <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>in</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> of carbon monoxide per mole is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mn>676.5</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mo>-</mo><mn>110</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>110.5</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>676.5</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mo>-</mo><mn>110</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given: Heat of combustion of carbon and carbon monoxide are <math><mo>-</mo><mn>393.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mo>-</mo><mn>283.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo></math> respectively.

(i) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>+</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>393.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

(ii) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>&#8710;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mn>283.5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

To find the heat of formation of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CO</mi></math> per mole.
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math>
<math> <mrow> <mo>&#916;</mo> <mrow> <msub> <mtext>H</mtext> <mtext>f</mtext> </msub> </mrow> </mrow> </math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>CO</mi></math>

From equations (i) and (ii),
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>=-393.5-</mi><mfenced><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>283</mn><mo>.5</mo></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> </mrow> </math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>kJ</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

</details>

- **(A)** <math><mn>2.48</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>1.49</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

- **(D)** <math><mn>1.49</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>)</mo></math> bond<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>360</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>.

In <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow></mfenced></msub><mo>+</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></mrow></mfenced></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>620</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi></math>,

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow></mfenced></msub></math> bond<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>620</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>90</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>80</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>kJ</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>mol</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>,

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>.</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></mrow></mfenced></msub></math> bond<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>80</mn><mrow><mn>96</mn><mo>.48</mo></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.83</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi>eV</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>bond</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>.

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#955;</mi><mrow><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">h</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8491;</mi><mo>)</mo></mrow></msub></math> for rupture of</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">E</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>hc</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#955;</mi></mfrac></math>
The wave length required to break <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> bond<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>12408</mn><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.83</mo></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>14950</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#8491;</mi></math>,

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>1495</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>,

### Q80. JEE Main 2014 (12 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The standard enthalpy of formation $\left(\Delta_f \mathrm{H}^{\circ}{ }_{298}\right)$ for methane, $\mathrm{CH}_4$ is $-74.9 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$. In order to calculate the average energy given out in the formation of a $\mathrm{C}-\mathrm{H}$ bond from this it is necessary to know which one of the following?

**Options:**

- **(A)** The dissociation energy of the hydrogen molecule, $\mathrm{H}_2$.
- **(B)** The first four ionisation energies of carbon.
- **(C)** The dissociation energy of $\mathrm{H}_2$ and enthalpy and sublimation of carbon (graphite).
- **(D)** The first four ionisation energies of carbon and electron affinity of hydrogen.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) The dissociation energy of the hydrogen molecule, $\mathrm{H}_2$.**

**Detailed Solution:**

To calculate average enthalpy of $\mathrm{C}-\mathrm{H}$ bond in methane following informations are needed
(i) dissociation energy of $\mathrm{H}_2$ i.e.
$$
\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{H}_2(g) \longrightarrow \mathrm{H}(\mathrm{g}) ; \Delta \mathrm{H}=x(\text { suppose } \quad)
$$
(ii) Sublimation energy of $\mathrm{C}$ (graphite) to $\mathrm{C}(g)$ $\mathrm{C}($ graphite $) \longrightarrow \mathrm{C}(g) ; \Delta \mathrm{H}=y$ (Suppose) Given
$\mathrm{C}($ graphite $)+2 \mathrm{H}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \longrightarrow \mathrm{CH}_4(\mathrm{~g}) ; \Delta \mathrm{H}=75 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

</details>

### Q81. JEE Main 2014 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The standard enthalpy of formation of<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>46</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math>. If bond enthalpy of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>436</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math> and that of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mn>712</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math> , the average bond enthalpy of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi></math> bond in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>NH</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></math> is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>964</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>-</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1102</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>1056</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>352</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>352</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>kJ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>mol</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

For The Reaction

<math> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <msub> <mtext>N</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>g</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <msub> <mtext>H</mtext> <mn>2</mn> </msub> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>g</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>→</mo> <msub> <mtext>NH</mtext> <mn>3</mn> </msub> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mtext>g</mtext> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>;</mo> <mtext>Δ</mtext> <msubsup> <mtext>H</mtext> <mtext>f</mtext> <mo>∘</mo> </msubsup> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mn>4</mn> <mn>6</mn> <msup> <mo>KJ mol</mo> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math>

<math> <mrow> <mtext>Δ</mtext> <msubsup> <mo>H</mo> <mtext>f</mtext> <mo>∘</mo> </msubsup> <mo>=</mo> <mo>-</mo> <mn>4</mn> <mn>6</mn> <mo>=</mo> <mo>∑</mo> <mrow> <mtext>B.E</mtext> <mo>-</mo> <mo>∑</mo> <mtext>B.E</mtext> </mrow> </mrow> </math>
                              Reactants      Product

<math> <mrow> <mo>∑</mo> <mtext>B.E</mtext> <mo>=</mo> <mo>∑</mo> <mtext>B.E</mtext> <mo>+</mo> <mn>4</mn> <mn>6</mn> </mrow> </math>
Product        Reactants

               <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>7</mn> <mn>1</mn> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>+</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>2</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mfenced close=")" open="(" separators=","> <mrow> <mn>4</mn> <mn>3</mn> <mn>6</mn> </mrow> </mfenced> <mo>+</mo> <mn>4</mn> <mn>6</mn> </mrow> </math>

               = 356 + 654 + 46 = 1056

Averge B.E for N-H Bond <math> <mrow> <mo>=</mo> <mfrac> <mrow> <mn>1</mn> <mn>0</mn> <mn>5</mn> <mn>6</mn> </mrow> <mrow> <mn>3</mn> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo> <mn>3</mn> <mn>5</mn> <mn>2</mn> <msup> <mo>KJ mol</mo> <mrow> <mo>-</mo> <mn>1</mn> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> </math>

</details>

### Q82. JEE Main 2013 (25 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given that:
(i) $\Delta_{\mathrm{f}} \mathrm{H}^{\circ}$ of $\mathrm{N}_2 \mathrm{O}$ is $82 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
(ii) Bond energies of $\mathrm{N} \equiv \mathrm{N}, \mathrm{N}=\mathrm{N}, \mathrm{O}=\mathrm{O}$ and $\mathrm{N}=\mathrm{O}$ are $946,418,498$ and $607 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ respectively,
The resonance energy of $\mathrm{N}_2 \mathrm{O}$ is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-88 \mathrm{~kJ}$
- **(B)** $-66 \mathrm{~kJ}$
- **(C)** $-62 \mathrm{~kJ}$
- **(D)** $-44 \mathrm{~kJ}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $-88 \mathrm{~kJ}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{N}_2(\mathrm{~g})+\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{O}_2 \rightarrow \mathrm{N}_2 \mathrm{O}(\mathrm{g})$
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/U2KGxGAQGCXTKfpYR6UjgwZK5fF4hKKIV0d8EhzKpZw.original.fullsize.png">

$\begin{aligned}
\Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{f}}{ }^{\mathrm{o}}= & {[\text { Energy required for breaking of bonds }] } \\
& -[\text { Energy released for forming of bonds }] \\
= & \left(\Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{N} \equiv \mathrm{N}}+\frac{1}{2} \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{O}=\mathrm{O}}-\left(\Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{N}=\mathrm{N}}+\Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{N}=\mathrm{O}}\right)\right. \\
= & \left(946+\frac{1}{2} \times 498\right)-(418+607)=170 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \\
& \text { Resonance energy }=170-82=88 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q84. JEE Main 2013 (22 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The reaction $\mathrm{X} \rightarrow \mathrm{Y}$ is an exothermic reaction. Activation energy of the reaction for $\mathrm{X}$ into $\mathrm{Y}$ is $150 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$. Enthalpy of reaction is $135 \mathrm{~kJ}$ $\mathrm{mol}^{-1}$. The activation energy for the reverse reaction, $\mathrm{Y} \rightarrow \mathrm{X}$ will be :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $280 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $285 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $270 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $15 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $285 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$X \longrightarrow Y ; \Delta \mathrm{H}=-135 \mathrm{~kJ} / \mathrm{mol}$, $\mathrm{E}_a=150 \mathrm{~kJ} / \mathrm{mol}$ For an exothermic reaction
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{E}_{a(\mathrm{~F} . \mathrm{R} .)}=\Delta \mathrm{H}+\mathrm{E}_{a(\mathrm{~B} . \mathrm{R} .)}^{\prime} \\
& 150=-135+\mathrm{E}_{a a \text { (B.R.) }}^{\prime} \\
& \mathrm{E}_{a(\mathrm{~B} . \mathrm{R})}^{\prime}=285 \mathrm{~kJ} / \mathrm{mol}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q85. JEE Main 2013 (22 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

Given
Reaction
Energy Change
$\begin{array}{ll} & \text { (in kJ) } \\ \mathrm{Li}(\mathrm{s}) \rightarrow \mathrm{Li}(\mathrm{g}) & 161 \\ \mathrm{Li}(\mathrm{g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{Li}^{+}(\mathrm{g}) & 520 \\ \frac{1}{2} \mathrm{~F}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{F}(\mathrm{g}) & 77 \\ \mathrm{~F}(\mathrm{~g})+\mathrm{e}^{-} \rightarrow \mathrm{F}^{-}(\mathrm{g}) & \begin{array}{l}\text { (Electron gain } \\ \text { enthalpy) }\end{array} \\ \mathrm{Li}^{+}(\mathrm{g})+\mathrm{F}^{-}(\mathrm{g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{LiF}(\mathrm{s}) & -1047 \\ \mathrm{Li}(\mathrm{s})+\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{~F}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{LiF}(\mathrm{s}) & -617\end{array}$
Based on data provided, the value of electron gain enthalpy of fluorine would be :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-300 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $-350 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $-328 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $-228 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $-328 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Applying Hess's Law
$$
\begin{aligned}
\Delta_{\mathrm{f}} \mathrm{H}^{\circ}= & \Delta_{\text {sub }} \mathrm{H}+\frac{1}{2} \Delta_{\text {diss }} \mathrm{H}+\text { I.E. }+\text { E.A }+\Delta_{\text {lattice }} \mathrm{H} \\
& -617=161+520+77+\text { E.A. }+(-1047) \\
& \text { E.A. }=-617+289=-328 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \\
& \therefore \text { electron affinity of fluorine } \\
& =-328 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q86. JEE Main 2012 (19 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The enthalpy of neutralisation of $\mathrm{NH}_4 \mathrm{OH}$ with $\mathrm{HCl}$ is $-51.46 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ and the enthalpy of neutralisation of $\mathrm{NaOH}$ with $\mathrm{HCl}$ is $-55.90 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$. The enthalpy of ionisation of $\mathrm{NH}_4 \mathrm{OH}$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-107.36 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $-4.44 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $+107.36 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $+4.44 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $+4.44 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{HCl} \longrightarrow \mathrm{H}^{+}+\mathrm{Cl}^{-}$
Strong acid (Complete ionisation)
$$
\mathrm{NH}_4 \mathrm{OH} \rightleftharpoons \mathrm{NH}_4^{+}+\mathrm{OH}^{-}
$$
Weak base $\Delta \mathrm{H}=x \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
$$
\begin{gathered}
\mathrm{H}^{+}+\mathrm{OH}^{-} \longrightarrow \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O} \\
\Delta \mathrm{H}=-55.90 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}
\end{gathered}
$$
(from neutralisation of strong acid and strong base)
From equation (i), (ii) and (iii)
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{NH}_4 \mathrm{OH}+\mathrm{HCl} \longrightarrow \mathrm{NH}_4^{+}+\mathrm{Cl}^{-}+\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O} \\
& \Delta \mathrm{H}=-51.46 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \\
\therefore \quad & x+(-55.90)=-51.46 \\
& x=-51.46+55.90 \\
& =4.44 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}
\end{aligned}
$$
$\therefore$ Enthalpy of ionisation of
$$
\mathrm{NH}_4 \mathrm{OH}=4.44 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}
$$

</details>

### Q87. JEE Main 2012 (07 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

The electron affinity of chlorine is $3.7 \mathrm{eV} .1$ gram of chlorine is completely converted to $\mathrm{Cl}^{-}$ion in a gaseous state. $\left(1 \mathrm{eV}=23.06 \mathrm{kcal} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}\right)$. Energy released in the process is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4.8 \mathrm{kcal}$
- **(B)** $7.2 \mathrm{kcal}$
- **(C)** $8.2 \mathrm{kcal}$
- **(D)** $2.4 \mathrm{kcal}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $2.4 \mathrm{kcal}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Number of moles $=\frac{1}{35.5}$
Given, $1 \mathrm{eV}=23.06 \mathrm{kcal} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$
$3.7 \mathrm{eV}=3.7 \times 23.06 \mathrm{kcal} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}$
i.e. 1 mole realease energy $=3.7 \times 23.06 \mathrm{kcal}$
$\therefore$ Energy released
$$
=\frac{1}{35.5} \times 3.7 \times 23.06 \mathrm{kcal}=2.4 \mathrm{kcal}
$$

</details>

### Q88. JEE Main 2010

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The energy required to break one mole of $\mathrm{Cl}-\mathrm{Cl}$ bonds in $\mathrm{Cl}_2$ is $242 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$. The longest wavelength of light capable of breaking a single $\mathrm{Cl}-\mathrm{Cl}$ bond is $\left(\mathrm{c}=3 \times 10^8 \mathrm{~ms}^{-1}\right.$ and $\left.\mathrm{N}_{\mathrm{A}}=6.02 \times 10^{23} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\right)$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $594 \mathrm{~nm}$
- **(B)** $640 \mathrm{~nm}$
- **(C)** $700 \mathrm{~nm}$
- **(D)** $494 \mathrm{~nm}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) $494 \mathrm{~nm}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Energy required for $1 \mathrm{Cl}_2$ molecule $=\frac{242 \times 10^3}{\mathrm{~N}_{\mathrm{A}}}$ Joules.
This energy is contained in photon of wavelength ' $\lambda$ '.
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \frac{\mathrm{hc}}{\lambda}=\mathrm{E} \Rightarrow \frac{6.626 \times 10^{-34} \times 3 \times 10^8}{\lambda}=\frac{242 \times 10^3}{6.022 \times 10^{23}} \\
& \lambda=4947 Å \approx 494 \mathrm{~nm}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q89. JEE Main 2010

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The standard enthalpy of formation of $\mathrm{NH}_3$ is $-46.0 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$. If the enthalpy of formation of $\mathrm{H}_2$ from its atoms is $-436 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$ and that of $\mathrm{N}_2$ is $-712 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$, the average bond enthalpy of $\mathrm{N}-\mathrm{H}$ bond in $\mathrm{NH}_3$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-964 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $+352 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $+1056 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $-1102 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $+352 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Enthalpy of formation of $\mathrm{NH}_3=-46 \mathrm{~kJ} / \mathrm{mole}$
$$
\therefore \mathrm{N}_2+3 \mathrm{H}_2 \rightarrow 2 \mathrm{NH}_3 \quad \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{f}}=-2 \times 46 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}
$$
Bond breaking is endothermic and Bond formation is exothermic
Assuming ' $\mathrm{x}$ ' is the bond energy of $\mathrm{N}-\mathrm{H}$ bond $\left(\mathrm{kJ} \mathrm{mol}^{-1}\right)$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \therefore 712+(3 \times 436)-6 \mathrm{x}=-46 \times 2 \\
& \therefore \mathrm{x}=352 \mathrm{~kJ} / \mathrm{mol}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q90. JEE Main 2009

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

On the basis of the following thermochemical data: $\left(\Delta \mathrm{fG}^{\circ} \mathrm{H}_{\text {(aq) }}^{+}=0\right)$
$$
\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\ell) \rightarrow \mathrm{H}^{+}(\mathrm{aq})+\mathrm{OH}^{-}(\mathrm{aq}) ; \Delta \mathrm{H}=57.32 \mathrm{~kJ} \\
& \mathrm{H}_2(\mathrm{~g})+\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{O}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{O}(\ell) ; \Delta \mathrm{H}=-286.20 \mathrm{~kJ}
\end{aligned}
$$
The value of enthalpy of formation of $\mathrm{OH}^{-}$ion at $25^{\circ} \mathrm{C}$ is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-22.88 \mathrm{~kJ}$
- **(B)** $-228.88 \mathrm{~kJ}$
- **(C)** $+228.88 \mathrm{~kJ}$
- **(D)** $-343.52 \mathrm{~kJ}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $-228.88 \mathrm{~kJ}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

By adding the two given equations, we have
$$
\mathrm{H}_{2(\mathrm{~g})}+\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{O}_{2(\mathrm{~g})} \rightarrow \mathrm{H}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+}+\mathrm{OH}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{-} ; \Delta \mathrm{H}=-228.88 \mathrm{Kj}
$$
Here $\Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\circ}$ of $\mathrm{H}_{(\mathrm{aq})}^{+}=0$
$\therefore \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{f}}^{\circ}$ of $\mathrm{OH}^{-}=-228.88 \mathrm{~kJ}$

</details>

### Q91. JEE Main 2008

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `1`

Oxidising power of chlorine in aqueous solution can be determined by the parameters indicated below:
$$
\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{Cl}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \stackrel{\frac{1}{2} \Delta_{\mathrm{ass}} \mathrm{H}^{\ominus}}{\longrightarrow} \mathrm{Cl}(\mathrm{g}) \stackrel{\Delta_{\mathrm{eg} H^{\ominus}}}{\longrightarrow} \mathrm{Cl}^{-}(\mathrm{g}) \stackrel{\Delta_{\mathrm{m} \mathrm{on}^{H^{\ominus}}}^{\longrightarrow}}{\longrightarrow} \mathrm{Cl}^{-}(\mathrm{aq}) .
$$
The energy involved in the conversion of $\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{Cl}_2(\mathrm{~g})$ to $\mathrm{Cl}^{-}(\mathrm{g})$ (using the data, $\left.\Delta_{\text {diss }} H_{\mathrm{Cl}_2}^{\ominus}=240 \mathrm{kJmol}^{-1}, \Delta_{\mathrm{eg}} H_{\mathrm{cl}}^{\ominus}=-349 \mathrm{kJmol}^{-1}, \Delta_{\mathrm{hyd}} \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{Cl}}^{\ominus}=-381 \mathrm{kJmol}^{-1}\right)$ will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** $+152 \mathrm{kJmol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $-610 \mathrm{kJmol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $-850 \mathrm{kJmol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $+120 \mathrm{kJmol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $-610 \mathrm{kJmol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{aligned}
& \text { For the process } \frac{1}{2} \mathrm{Cl}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \longrightarrow \mathrm{Cl}_{\mathrm{aq}}^{-} \\
& \Delta \mathrm{H}=\frac{1}{2} \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\text {diss }} \text { of } \mathrm{Cl}_2+\Delta_{\mathrm{eg}} \mathrm{Cl}+\Delta_{\mathrm{hyd}} \mathrm{Cl}^{-} \\
& =+\frac{240}{2}-349-381 \\
& =-610 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q92. JEE Main 2006

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

The enthalpy changes for the following processes are listed below:
$\begin{array}{ll}\mathrm{Cl}_2(\mathrm{~g})=2 \mathrm{Cl}(\mathrm{g}), & 242.3 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \\ \mathrm{I}_2(\mathrm{~g})=2 \mathrm{I}(\mathrm{g}), & 151.0 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \\ \mathrm{ICl}(\mathrm{g})=\mathrm{I}(\mathrm{g})+\mathrm{Cl}(\mathrm{g}), & 211.3 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1} \\ \mathrm{I}_2(\mathrm{~s})=\mathrm{I}_2(\mathrm{~g}), & 62.76 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}\end{array}$
Given that the standard states for iodine and chlorine are $\mathrm{I}_2(\mathrm{~s})$ and $\mathrm{Cl}_2(\mathrm{~g})$, the standard enthalpy of formation for $\mathrm{ICl}(\mathrm{g})$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-14.6 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $-16.8 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $+16.8 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** $+244.8 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $+16.8 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{aligned}
& \frac{1}{2} \mathrm{I}_2(\mathrm{~s})+\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{Cl}_2 \longrightarrow \mathrm{ICl}(\mathrm{g}) \\
& \Delta \mathrm{H}=\left[\frac{1}{2} \Delta \mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{b}_2(s) \rightarrow k_2(g)}+\frac{1}{2} \mu_{1-1}+\frac{1}{2} \mu_{\mathrm{cl}-\mathrm{cl}}\right]-\left[\mu_{1-a}\right] \\
& =\left(\frac{1}{2} \times 62.76+\frac{1}{2} \times 151.0+\frac{1}{2} \times 242.3\right)-(211.3) \\
& =228.03-211.3 \\
& \Delta \mathrm{H}=16.73 \\
&
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q94. JEE Main 2005

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `3`

If the bond dissociation energies of $X Y, X_2$ and $Y_2$ (all diatomic molecules) are in the ratio of 1:1:0.5 and $\Delta_t \mathrm{H}$ for the formation of $X Y$ is $-200 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mole}^{-1}$. The bond dissociation energy of $\mathrm{X}_2$ will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** $100 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(B)** $200 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(C)** $300 \mathrm{~kJ} \mathrm{~mol}^{-1}$
- **(D)** None of these

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) None of these**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\begin{aligned}
& X Y \longrightarrow X_{(g)}+Y_{(g)} ; \quad \Delta \mathrm{H}=+\mathrm{a} \mathrm{kJ} / \mathrm{mole} \ldots \ldots \ldots \ldots . .(\mathrm{i}) \\
& \mathrm{X}_2 \longrightarrow 2 \mathrm{X} ; \quad \Delta \mathrm{H}=+\mathrm{a} \mathrm{kJ} / \mathrm{mole} \ldots \ldots \ldots \ldots \ldots \text { (ii) } \\
& \mathrm{Y}_2 \longrightarrow 2 \mathrm{Y} ; \quad \Delta \mathrm{H}=+0.5 \mathrm{a} \mathrm{kJ} / \mathrm{mole} \ldots \ldots \ldots \ldots . \text { (iii) } \\
& \frac{1}{2} \times\left. \text { (ii) }+\frac{1}{2} \times \text { (iii) }-\right.\text { (i), Gives } \\
& \frac{1}{2} \mathrm{X}_2+\frac{1}{2} \mathrm{Y}_2 \longrightarrow \mathrm{XY} ; \quad \Delta \mathrm{H}=\left(+\frac{\mathrm{a}}{2}+\frac{0.5}{2} \mathrm{a}-\mathrm{a}\right) \mathrm{kJ} / \mathrm{mole} \\
& +\frac{\mathrm{a}}{2}+\frac{0.5 \mathrm{a}}{2}-\mathrm{a}=-200 \\
& \mathrm{a}=800 .
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q100. JEE Main 2003

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Difficulty Level:* `2`

If at $298 \mathrm{~K}$ the bond energies of $\mathrm{C}-\mathrm{H}, \mathrm{C}-\mathrm{C}, \mathrm{C}=\mathrm{C}$ and $\mathrm{H}-\mathrm{H}$ bonds are respectively $414,347,615$ and $435 \mathrm{~kJ}$ $\mathrm{mol}^{-1}$, the value of enthalpy change for the reaction $\mathrm{H}_2 \mathrm{C}=\mathrm{CH}_2(\mathrm{~g})+\mathrm{H}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{H}_3 \mathrm{C}-\mathrm{CH}_3(\mathrm{~g})$ at $298 \mathrm{~K}$ will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-250 \mathrm{~kJ}$
- **(B)** $+125 \mathrm{~kJ}$
- **(C)** $-125 \mathrm{~kJ}$
- **(D)** $+250 \mathrm{~kJ}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $-125 \mathrm{~kJ}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{CH}_2=\mathrm{CH}_2(\mathrm{~g})+\mathrm{H}_2(\mathrm{~g}) \rightarrow \mathrm{CH}_3-\mathrm{CH}_3$
$\Delta H=1(\mathrm{C}=\mathrm{C})+4(\mathrm{C}-\mathrm{H})+1(\mathrm{H}-\mathrm{H})-1(\mathrm{C}-\mathrm{C})-6(\mathrm{C}-\mathrm{H})=1(\mathrm{C}=\mathrm{C})+1(\mathrm{H}-\mathrm{H})-1(\mathrm{C}-\mathrm{C})-2(\mathrm{C}-\mathrm{H})$
$=615+435-347-2 \times 414=1050-1175=-125 \mathrm{~kJ}$

</details>
